# IS-CIRQ-P-024-G: Continual Improvement Policy

**Document: IS-CIRQ-P-024-G**

**Standards Name: Continual Improvement Policy**

**Category: ISMS Support Process**

**Division: Policy**

**Standard Retention: Exist and No Corrections**

**Standard Type: Global**

**Version:** 1.0 **Effective Date:** 2025-07-01 **Review Date:**
2026-07-01 **Approved By:** Executive Committee

**1. Purpose**

The purpose of this policy is to establish Cirque Corporation\'s
commitment to the continual improvement of its Information Security
Management System (ISMS). This policy ensures that the ISMS remains
effective, suitable, and adequate in managing information security risks
and opportunities in a dynamic environment, constantly striving for
enhanced performance. This policy aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clause
10.2 (Continual improvement).

**2. Scope**

This policy applies to all processes, activities, and personnel involved
in the operation and enhancement of Cirque Corporation\'s ISMS across
all global operations (US, Taipei, China). It covers all initiatives
aimed at improving the overall information security posture and the
effectiveness of the ISMS.

**3. Definitions**

-   **Continual Improvement:** A recurring activity to enhance
    performance. In the context of the ISMS, it refers to ongoing
    efforts to improve the suitability, adequacy, and effectiveness of
    the information security system.

-   **ISMS:** Information Security Management System.

-   **PDCA Cycle (Plan-Do-Check-Act):** A cyclical process for
    continuous improvement, used as the underlying methodology for the
    ISMS.

**4. Policy Requirements**

**4.1. Commitment to Continual Improvement:** a. Cirque Corporation is
committed to continually improving the suitability, adequacy, and
effectiveness of its ISMS. b. This commitment is demonstrated through
the allocation of necessary resources, management review, and the
implementation of identified improvements.

**4.2. Improvement Methodology (PDCA Cycle):** a. Cirque Corporation
shall utilize the Plan-Do-Check-Act (PDCA) cycle as the foundation for
its continual improvement efforts: \* **PLAN:** Establish information
security objectives and processes necessary to deliver results in
accordance with Cirque Corporation\'s overall information security
policy. (e.g., Risk assessment, ISMS planning). \* **DO:** Implement the
information security policy, controls, and processes. (e.g., Control
implementation, operations). \* **CHECK:** Monitor, measure, and review
performance against information security policy and objectives, and
report results. (e.g., Monitoring, internal audits, management reviews).
\* **ACT:** Take corrective actions and implement improvements based on
the results of the check phase. (e.g., Corrective actions, ISMS
updates).

**4.3. Sources of Improvement:** a. Inputs for continual improvement
shall be systematically gathered from various sources, including but not
limited to: \* Results of IS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement,
Analysis, and Evaluation Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance
Monitoring Procedure. \* Outcomes of IS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit
Procedure and IS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template. \*
Decisions and actions from IS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy and
IS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure. \* Nonconformities and
the results of IS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action
Policy and IS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure
(IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form). \* Information
security incidents and their post-incident reviews. \* Changes in
internal and external issues (e.g., new threats, technologies, legal
requirements). \* Feedback from interested parties (e.g., customers,
employees, regulators, suppliers). \* Suggestions from employees and
subject matter experts.

**4.4. Planning and Implementation of Improvements:** a. Opportunities
for improvement shall be assessed, prioritized based on risk and
benefit, and documented. b. An action plan, including responsibilities,
timelines, and necessary resources, shall be developed for each
significant improvement initiative. c. Implemented improvements shall be
monitored and evaluated to confirm their effectiveness and contribution
to the ISMS.

**4.5. Documentation and Communication:** a. Records of continual
improvement activities, including identified opportunities, action
plans, implementation status, and verification of effectiveness, shall
be maintained. b. The outcomes of improvement initiatives shall be
communicated to relevant stakeholders as appropriate.

**5. Responsibilities**

-   **Executive Committee:** Demonstrates leadership and commitment to
    continual improvement by providing resources, reviewing improvement
    progress, and making strategic decisions during management reviews.

-   **ISMS Owner (e.g., Information Security Officer/IT Manager):**
    Champions continual improvement, oversees the collection and
    analysis of improvement inputs, facilitates the planning and
    tracking of improvement initiatives, and reports on progress to
    management.

-   **Process Owners:** Responsible for identifying and implementing
    improvements within their respective ISMS processes and controls.

-   **All Personnel:** Are encouraged to identify and report
    opportunities for improvement related to information security.

**6. Related Documents**

-   IS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy

-   IS-CIRQ-P-021-G: Monitoring, Measurement, Analysis, and Evaluation
    Policy

-   IS-CIRQ-PR-022-G: ISMS Performance Monitoring Procedure

-   IS-CIRQ-PR-023-G: Internal Audit Procedure

-   IS-CIRQ-F-006-G: Internal Audit Report Template

-   IS-CIRQ-P-022-G: Management Review Policy

-   IS-CIRQ-PR-024-G: Management Review Procedure

-   IS-CIRQ-F-007-G: Management Review Meeting Minutes Template

-   IS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy

-   IS-CIRQ-PR-025-G: Corrective Action Procedure

-   IS-CIRQ-F-008-G: Corrective Action Request (CAR) Form

**7. Policy Review**

This policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are
significant changes to Cirque Corporation\'s ISMS, business operations,
or the information security landscape, to ensure its ongoing relevance
and effectiveness.

# IS-CIRQ-P-025-G: Employee Termination Process

**Document: IS-CIRQ-P-024-G**

**Standards Name: Continual Improvement Policy**

**Category: ISMS Support Process**

**Division: Policy**

**Standard Retention: Exist and No Corrections**

**Standard Type: Global**

**Version:** 1.0 **Effective Date:** 2025-07-01 **Review Date:**
2026-07-01 **Approved By:** Executive Committee

**1. Purpose**

The purpose of this policy is to define Cirque Corporation's
standardized process for the termination of employees, contractors, and
temporary personnel to ensure the orderly separation of individuals from
the organization while protecting company assets, information, and
systems. This process supports the confidentiality, integrity, and
availability of information and aligns with ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Clauses
6.3, 7.5, and Annex A controls related to human resource security and
access management.

**2. Scope**

This policy applies to all Cirque Corporation personnel, including
full-time employees, part-time employees, contractors, consultants, and
temporary workers, across all global operations (US, Taipei, China). It
covers voluntary and involuntary terminations, role separations, and
contract expirations.

**3. Definitions**

**Termination**: The formal end of an individual's employment or
contractual relationship with Cirque Corporation, whether voluntary or
involuntary.\
• **Access** **Rights**: Logical and physical permissions granted to
personnel, including system accounts, credentials, and facility access.\
• **Company Assets**: Any physical or digital property owned or licensed
by Cirque Corporation, including hardware, software, data,
documentation, and intellectual property.\
• **Exit Process**: The coordinated set of activities required to ensure
secure and compliant employee separation.4. Policy Requirements

**4.1. General Termination Principles**

a\. All terminations shall be handled in a consistent, professional, and
legally compliant manner.\
b. Termination activities shall be coordinated between Human Resources
(HR), Information Technology (IT), the employee's manager, and other
relevant stakeholders.\
c. Termination actions shall be executed promptly to minimize
operational and information security risks.

**4.2. Notification and Authorization**

a\. HR shall formally initiate the termination process upon approval
from authorized management.\
b. IT and relevant process owners shall be notified in advance of the
termination date when feasible, or immediately in cases of involuntary
or high-risk terminations.\
c. The termination date and time shall be clearly communicated to all
responsible parties.

**4.3. Access Revocation**

a\. All logical access rights (e.g., network accounts, email, VPN, cloud
services, applications) shall be disabled or revoked no later than the
effective termination date and time.\
b. Physical access (e.g., badges, keys, facility access) shall be
revoked concurrently with logical access.\
c. Privileged or administrative access shall be revoked immediately upon
termination notification.

**4.4. Asset Recovery and Information Protection**

a\. All company assets issued to the individual shall be identified,
collected, and accounted for, including but not limited to laptops,
mobile devices, ID badges, documents, and storage media.\
b. Confidential information, intellectual property, and proprietary
materials shall be returned or securely retained by Cirque Corporation.\
c. Any data stored on personal or third-party devices (if permitted)
shall be returned or securely removed in accordance with company policy.

**4.5. Knowledge Transfer and Exit Activities**

a\. When applicable, managers shall ensure that critical knowledge,
documentation, and responsibilities are transitioned prior to the
termination date.\
b. HR may conduct an exit interview to gather feedback and reinforce
ongoing confidentiality obligations.\
c. Continuing obligations related to confidentiality, intellectual
property, and non-disclosure agreements shall be reaffirmed to the
departing individual.

**4.6. Documentation and Recordkeeping**

a\. All termination activities shall be documented and retained in
accordance with Cirque Corporation's record retention requirements.\
b. Evidence of access revocation and asset recovery shall be maintained
for audit and compliance purposes.

**5. Responsibilities**

Executive Committee: Provides oversight and ensures adequate resources
and authority for effective termination controls.\
• Human Resources: Initiates and coordinates the termination process,
ensures legal and policy compliance, and maintains personnel records.\
• IT / ISMS Owner: Ensures timely revocation of system access,
protection of information assets, and verification of completion.\
• Managers: Coordinate operational handover, identify assigned assets,
and support HR and IT during the termination process.\
• Departing Personnel: Return all company assets and comply with ongoing
confidentiality and contractual obligations.

**6. Related Documents**

• IS-CIRQ-P-001-G: Information Security Policy\
• IS-CIRQ-P-003-G: Access Control Policy\
• IS-CIRQ-PR-004-G: User Access Management Procedure\
• IS-CIRQ-P-006-G: Asset Management Policy\
• IS-CIRQ-PR-009-G: Acceptable Use Procedure\
• IS-CIRQ-P-023-G: Nonconformity and Corrective Action Policy

**7. Policy Review**

This policy will be reviewed at least annually, or sooner if there are
significant changes to Cirque Corporation\'s ISMS, business operations,
or the information security landscape, to ensure its ongoing relevance
and effectiveness.
